    "oldString": "\\footnote{We also tested DeBERTa-v3-large (45.17\\% standalone), which yields $+4.83$\\,pp gain on Mistral+PW ($w{=}5$, $p{=}0.11$)---consistent with the capability gap hypothesis's prediction (gap$=+5.84$, predicted gain $\\approx+5$\\,pp). See Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:cross_nli_scale}.}",
    "newString": "\\footnote{DeBERTa-v3-large (45.17\\% standalone) yields $+4.83$\\,pp on Mistral+PW ($w{=}5$, $p{=}0.11$), matching the predicted ${\\sim}+5$\\,pp (gap$=+5.84$); see Table~\\ref{tab:cross_nli_scale}.}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\nThe analyses in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\\S\\ref{sec:remediation} confirm all three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's bias. The oracle gap (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test this with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \\textsc{entailment}, \\textsc{contradiction}, or \\textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \\textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\\%) and performs poorly on \\textsc{Unknown} (3.5\\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThe 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). This produces per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} problems (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%) where the LLM is weakest, while SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested---DeBERTa, RoBERTa, and BART (all MNLI-trained)---produce significant improvements on ProofWriter at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}), confirming the effect is not verifier-specific.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nCross-architecture verification succeeds when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline. On ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33\\%), enabling $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\\%) far exceeds DeBERTa (49.51\\%), the combo yields $\\Delta \\leq +0.49$\\,pp (all $p = 1.0$). Aggregation gain shrinks with model capability: $+8.01$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; Appendix~\\ref{app:single-trace}). Across all conditions at $w{=}3$, combo gain tracks the capability gap (Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}): meaningful improvement emerges only when the verifier's standalone accuracy approaches or exceeds the generator's SC baseline.\\footnote{We also tested DeBERTa-v3-large (45.17\\% standalone), which yields $+4.83$\\,pp gain on Mistral+PW ($w{=}5$, $p{=}0.11$)---consistent with the capability gap hypothesis's prediction (gap$=+5.84$, predicted gain $\\approx+5$\\,pp). See Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:cross_nli_scale}.}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Meaningful gain emerges when the gap is positive (verifier exceeds generator).}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-5mm}\n\\end{figure}\n",

    "newString": "\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\nThe analyses in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\\S\\ref{sec:remediation} confirm all three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's bias. The oracle gap (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test this with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \\textsc{entailment}, \\textsc{contradiction}, or \\textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \\textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\\%) and performs poorly on \\textsc{Unknown} (3.5\\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThe 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). This produces per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} problems (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%) where the LLM is weakest, while SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested---DeBERTa, RoBERTa, and BART (all MNLI-trained)---produce significant improvements on ProofWriter at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}), confirming the effect is not verifier-specific.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nCross-architecture verification succeeds when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline. On ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33\\%), enabling $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\\%) far exceeds DeBERTa (49.51\\%), the combo yields $\\Delta \\leq +0.49$\\,pp (all $p = 1.0$). Aggregation gain shrinks with model capability: $+8.01$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; Appendix~\\ref{app:single-trace}). Across all conditions at $w{=}3$, combo gain tracks the capability gap (Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}): meaningful improvement emerges only when the verifier's standalone accuracy approaches or exceeds the generator's SC baseline.\\footnote{DeBERTa-v3-large (45.17\\% standalone) yields $+4.83$\\,pp on Mistral+PW ($w{=}5$, $p{=}0.11$), matching the predicted ${\\sim}+5$\\,pp (gap$=+5.84$); see Table~\\ref{tab:cross_nli_scale}.}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Meaningful gain emerges when the gap is positive (verifier exceeds generator).}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-5mm}\n\\end{figure}\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nThe Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: the \\emph{direct} limits voting diversity ($K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$), while the \\emph{indirect} collapses MAX-SAT to vote counting (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\\times$ LLaMA) aligns with regime boundaries; thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) confirms this scales with capability. For practitioners, $C_0$ with SC accuracy predicts when augmentation will fail. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Our extensions of prior negative results \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---provide reusable diagnostics for any trace-aggregation system.\n",

Actually, the cleanest way is to add `\enlargethispage{1\baselineskip}` somewhere in the content that appears on page 8.

    "originalFile": "\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nThe Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: the \\emph{direct} limits voting diversity ($K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$), while the \\emph{indirect} collapses MAX-SAT to vote counting (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\\times$ LLaMA) aligns with regime boundaries; thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) confirms this scales with capability. For practitioners, $C_0$ with SC accuracy predicts when augmentation will fail. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Our extensions of prior negative results \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---provide reusable diagnostics for any trace-aggregation system.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\vspace{-1mm}\n\\section{Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted symbolic constraints project the generating model's biases rather than providing independent verification, explaining why twenty-plus remediation strategies cannot break the same-model ceiling. Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; a cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage entirely. For practitioners, architecturally independent signals remain necessary for reliable constraint-based reasoning. The two DeBERTa variants use different training checkpoints (cross-encoder/nli-deberta-base vs.\ MoritzLaurer/DeBERTa-v3-large-mnli-fever-anli-ling-wanli), which accounts for the counterintuitive standalone accuracy difference.}

[Director 指令] [Patch — V10 R8 Reviewer Response, 5 patches] 以下全部基于 R8 审稿反馈的 must-fix 和 should-fix。按优先级执行。

**Patch A (W1 Critical): FOL → symbolic terminology 通篇替换**
- 搜索全文 "FOL" / "first-order logic"，替换为 "symbolic" / "logical" / "symbolic constraints"
- 保留 "FOL-style" 仅在描述 Z3 接口实现细节处（method section 中 1-2 次）
- 这是 terminology 统一，不改内容含义

**Patch B (W2 Critical): Cross-Generator NLI Combo Table 加入 §6**
在 §6 NLI Escape 讨论中加 1 个 Table（或扩展现有 table），展示 cross-generator + cross-verifier 完整矩阵：

| Generator | SC Baseline | NLI Verifier | NLI Standalone | Gap | Combo Δ (w=5) | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mistral-7B | 39.33% | DeBERTa-base | 55.00% | +15.67 | +10.67pp | <0.0001 |
| Mistral-7B | 39.33% | BART-large | 46.20% | +6.87 | +7.17pp | 0.0001 |
| Mistral-7B | 39.33% | RoBERTa-large | 37.70% | -1.63 | +6.50pp | 0.0002 |
| Mistral-7B | 39.33% | DeBERTa-v3-large | 45.17% | +5.84 | +4.83pp | 0.11 |
| Qwen3-14B | 85.00% | DeBERTa-base | 36.17% | -48.83 | +0.33pp | 0.83 |
| Qwen3-14B | 85.00% | DeBERTa-v3-large | 45.17% | -39.83 | -0.67pp | 0.80 |

在 table 后加 1 段解读：
- "Cross-generator scaling confirms the Capability Gap Hypothesis: combo gain is positive and significant only when verifier standalone accuracy exceeds or approaches the generator's SC baseline (rows 1-3). When the generator substantially outperforms the verifier (rows 5-6, Qwen3-14B SC 85% vs. DeBERTa ≤45%), NLI votes are overwhelmed by SC majority and provide no gain."
- Qualify §6 conclusion: 把现有 "NLI escape works" 限定为 "preliminary signal strongest on weaker generators (Mistral-7B); Capability Gap Corollary predicts when cross-architecture verification can and cannot help."

同时把之前 D126 的 appendix table 合并进这个 main-text table（不再需要单独 appendix table）。

**Patch C (W3 Should): §2 Related Work 重定位 novelty**
在 Related Work 中关于 self-correction failures 的讨论后，加 1-2 段 strong differentiation：
- "While prior work (Huang et al. 2024; Stechly et al. 2024; Amonkar & Mukherjee 2024; Chan et al. 2024) documents that LLM self-correction fails, these studies test informal correction (natural-language feedback loops). We test the stronger form: deterministic symbolic constraints extracted into FOL and verified via Z3 MAX-SAT — and show that even this stronger verification inherits model bias."
- "Beyond documenting failure, we provide quantitative diagnostic tools — Bias Ratio (BR), Effective Independence (Eff-K), conclusion leakage rate (C_D), and the Capability Gap Corollary — that explain WHY symbolic aggregation fails and WHEN cross-architecture verification can overcome the ceiling. These analytical contributions are novel to this line of work."

**Patch D (W4 Should): Abstract scope 限定**
替换 "across the full LLM capability range" → "across four open-source LLMs (7B–32B) on formal and logical reasoning benchmarks"

**Patch E (W5 Should): §3 添加 Turpin et al. connection**
在 §3 Symbolic Confirmation Bias (BR) 讨论后加 1 段：
- "The observed BR of 4.97× may partly reflect unfaithful chain-of-thought reasoning (Turpin et al., 2024): if the model's stated reasoning does not reflect its true decision process, the extracted symbolic constraints are projections of a hidden bias rather than independent verification steps. This connects our symbolic-level finding to the broader CoT faithfulness literature."
- 如果 references.bib 中没有 Turpin 2024，添加：Turpin, M., Michael, J., Perez, E., & Bowman, S. R. (2024). Language Models Don't Always Say What They Think: Unfaithful Explanations in Chain-of-Thought Prompting. NeurIPS 2023.

**页数预算**：Patch B 增加约 0.3 页（1 table + 1 段），Patch C 增加 ~0.2 页。如需空间，从 §4 SICA exhaustion 压缩（8 个否定方向可合并精简表述）。目标 ≤ 8 页 main text。

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into FOL for external provers; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors. Recent extensions---CLOVER \citep{ryu2025clover}, DyLoS \citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \citep{wang2025fover}, VERGE \citep{singh2026verge}, LogicAgent \citep{luo2025logicagent}---add compositional translation, solver selection, decomposition, trained verifiers, and formal refinement, each introducing capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue that logical soundness is not a reliable criterion for neurosymbolic verification with LLMs. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, paralleling our confirmation bias finding (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. We extend this by quantifying \emph{why} self-correction fails---same-model traces are correlated voters ($K_{\text{eff}}$ collapses)---and showing this persists even under formal verification (Z3-checked FOL) and at the process level (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	The analyses in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\S\ref{sec:remediation} confirm all three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}): self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's bias. The oracle gap (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the model's distribution.
6	
7	\paragraph{Method.}
8	We test this with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \textsc{entailment}, \textsc{contradiction}, or \textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.
9	
10	\paragraph{Main result.}
11	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\%) and performs poorly on \textsc{Unknown} (3.5\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.
12	
13	\paragraph{Why it works.}
14	The 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). This produces per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \textsc{False} problems (71.43\% vs.\ SC's 38.10\%) where the LLM is weakest, while SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures tested---DeBERTa, RoBERTa, and BART (all MNLI-trained)---produce significant improvements on ProofWriter at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}), confirming the effect is not verifier-specific.
15	
16	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
17	Cross-architecture verification succeeds when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline. On ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33\%), enabling $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\%) far exceeds DeBERTa (49.51\%), the combo yields $\Delta \leq +0.49$\,pp (all $p = 1.0$). Aggregation gain shrinks with model capability: $+8.01$\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; Appendix~\ref{app:single-trace}). Across all conditions at $w{=}3$, combo gain tracks the capability gap (Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}): meaningful improvement emerges only when the verifier's standalone accuracy approaches or exceeds the generator's SC baseline.
18	
19	\begin{figure}[t]
20	\centering
21	\includegraphics[width=0.82\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}
22	\vspace{-3mm}
23	\caption{Combo gain vs.\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Meaningful gain emerges when the gap is positive (verifier exceeds generator).}
24	\label{fig:capability-gap}
25	\vspace{-6mm}
26	\end{figure}
27	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Mr9JJj5FL4Ku3DrSNS9Vdw`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	The Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: the \emph{direct} limits voting diversity ($K_{\text{eff}} < 2$), while the \emph{indirect} collapses MAX-SAT to vote counting (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\times$ LLaMA) aligns with regime boundaries; thinking-mode degeneration ($\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) confirms this scales with capability. For practitioners, $C_0$ with SC accuracy predicts when augmentation will fail. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Our extensions of prior negative results \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---provide reusable diagnostics for any trace-aggregation system.
5	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01USJV8P3SXYmrR6FSTzpew5`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic (FOL) constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across the full LLM capability range---from Mistral-7B (54\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86\% SC)---and three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect; TOST equivalence tests (margin $\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for four conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the oracle ceiling.
8	However, a cross-architecture proof-of-concept---DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC---yields $+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures, suggesting that the oracle gap is partially recoverable when verification signal comes from outside the reasoning model's distribution.
9	
```

### Tool result

_source line 14458 · 2026-05-20T20:12:30.185Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Amh64q7e96z9B7JyFTbceX`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
8	
9	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
10	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
11	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
12	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
13	
14	We ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \citep{austensmith1996}.
15	When independence fails \citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.
16	Measuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
17	The bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.
18	Chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
19	When the same model extracts FOL constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
20	Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.
21	
22	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
23	We evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
24	Twenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.
25	While prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.
26	
27	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: same-model gold FOL yields $+29.90$\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the same-model ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
28	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
29	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
30	
31	Our contributions are:
32	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
33	\item \textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\kappa \geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter, $p = 0.013$; confirmed across three NLI architectures) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\S\ref{sec:remediation}, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
34	
35	\item \textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
36	
37	\item \textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
38	\end{enumerate}
39	
```

introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts FOL constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.\nWhile prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: same-model gold FOL yields $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the same-model ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter, $p = 0.013$; confirmed across three NLI architectures) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
      "numLines": 39,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 39
    }

1	\section{Background \& Setup}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}
5	\label{sec:condorcet}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \to \infty$ \citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = \frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \cdot \kappa}.
11	\end{equation}
12	In our experiments (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. Note that $\kappa$ is a qualitative proxy for the intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC); we use $K_{\text{eff}}$ as an interpretive indicator rather than a precise statistical estimator. This \emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.
13	
14	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
15	\label{sec:pipeline}
16	
17	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
18	
19	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
20	
21	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts FOL constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}). The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
22	
23	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence; each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:
24	\begin{equation}
25	\label{eq:maxsat}
26	\mathcal{C}^* = \arg\max_{\mathcal{S} \subseteq \mathcal{C}} \sum_{c \in \mathcal{S}} w_c \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \mathcal{S} \text{ is satisfiable.}
27	\end{equation}
28	Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\texttt{answer\_a} == \texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.
29	
30	\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\mathcal{C}^{\setminus} = \mathcal{C} \setminus \mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:
31	\begin{equation}
32	\label{eq:score}
33	\text{score}(a) = \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^*} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a) - \alpha \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^{\setminus}} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a),
34	\end{equation}
35	where $\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \arg\max_{a} \text{score}(a)$.
36	
37	\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:
38	\begin{equation}
39	\label{eq:degenerate}
40	\text{score}(a) \approx \sum_{\substack{c \in \mathcal{C}^* \\ \text{source}(c) \to a}} w_c,
41	\end{equation}
42	equivalent to majority voting. We show in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.
43	
44	\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\{q : |\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\}| / |\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC. The penalty weight $\alpha = 0.5$ is not a critical hyperparameter: accuracy varies by at most $0.49$\,pp across $\alpha \in \{0.0, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\}$ on Mistral-7B FOLIO.
45	
46	\begin{figure}[t]
47	\centering
48	\small
49	\begin{tikzpicture}[
50	    node distance=0.4cm and 0.35cm,
51	    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.6cm,
52	                text width=1.6cm, align=center, font=\scriptsize},
53	    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},
54	    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},
55	    lbl/.style={font=\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},
56	    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,
57	                    inner sep=3pt},
58	]
59	\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};
60	\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\traces};
61	\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract FOL\\constraints};
62	\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\ +\\weight};
63	\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\(Z3)};
64	
65	\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};
66	\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\vote};
67	
68	\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);
69	\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);
70	\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);
71	\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);
72	\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);
73	\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);
74	
75	\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};
76	
77	\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\scriptsize\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};
78	
79	\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};
80	\end{tikzpicture}
81	\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}
82	\label{fig:pipeline}
83	\end{figure}
84	
85	\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}
86	\label{sec:mirage}
87	
88	We hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\S\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \emph{Bias Ratio}:
89	\begin{equation}
90	\label{eq:mirage}
91	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
92	\end{equation}
93	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}; Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Together with the oracle gap (\S\ref{sec:oracle}), BR bounds improvement: self-extracted constraints capture at most a $1/\text{BR}$ fraction of the oracle ceiling ($\Delta_{\max} \lesssim 6.0$\,pp for Mistral-7B, $\lesssim 3.6$\,pp for Qwen-14B; observed mean across conditions: $+2.12$\,pp). Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\S\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
94

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/limitations_content.tex:2:[Omitted long matching line]

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/appendix.tex:227:[Omitted long matching line]
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/appendix.tex:537:[Omitted long matching line]
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/appendix.tex:735:[Omitted long matching line]
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex:19:When the same model extracts FOL constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex:22:We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex:27:Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: same-model gold FOL yields $+29.90$\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the same-model ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).

    "newString": "which extracts symbolic constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.",
    "originalFile": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic (FOL) constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross the full LLM capability range---from Mistral-7B (54\\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86\\% SC)---and three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect; TOST equivalence tests (margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for four conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nHowever, a cross-architecture proof-of-concept---DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC---yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures, suggesting that the oracle gap is partially recoverable when verification signal comes from outside the reasoning model's distribution.\n",

    "newString": "When the same model extracts symbolic constraints from these correlated traces",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts FOL constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.\nWhile prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: same-model gold FOL yields $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the same-model ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter, $p = 0.013$; confirmed across three NLI architectures) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",

recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter, $p = 0.013$; confirmed across three NLI architectures) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 19,
        "oldLines": 7,
        "newStart": 19,
        "newLines": 7,
        "lines": [
          " When the same model extracts symbolic constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).",
          " Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.",
          " ",
          "-We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).",
          "+We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract symbolic constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).",
          " We evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).",
          " Twenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.",
          " While prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics."
        ]
      }

    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into FOL for external provers; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors. Recent extensions---CLOVER \\citep{ryu2025clover}, DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, VERGE \\citep{singh2026verge}, LogicAgent \\citep{luo2025logicagent}---add compositional translation, solver selection, decomposition, trained verifiers, and formal refinement, each introducing capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue that logical soundness is not a reliable criterion for neurosymbolic verification with LLMs. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, paralleling our confirmation bias finding (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. We extend this by quantifying \\emph{why} self-correction fails---same-model traces are correlated voters ($K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapses)---and showing this persists even under formal verification (Z3-checked FOL) and at the process level (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).",

Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).",

11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold FOL annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K~\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models on three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations across ${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances. Beyond accuracy comparisons, we conduct diagnostic analyses of symbolic confirmation bias and process-level trace independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold FOL annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$; extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$. Z3 timeout: 10s. $\\alpha{=}0.5$. Statistical significance via McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs from 10,000 resamples for $n \\geq 200$.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational cost.} On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($K{=}12$), SICA takes $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock time; constraint extraction ($K$ additional LLM calls) accounts for 76\\% of overhead, Z3 solving is negligible (${<}0.1\\%$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline. We identify three empirical regimes (thresholds observation-driven, not theoretically derived). SICA effectiveness depends sharply on this accuracy regime.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions sorted by SC accuracy. 66	\paragraph{Intermediate-SC region.} Mistral-7B on FOLIO (SC $\sim$50--55\%) shows positive directional trends in 2 of 4 seeds ($\Delta = +3.43$\,pp, $p = 0.092$; $+2.94$\,pp, $p = 0.070$); no seed reaches $p < 0.05$ (4-seed mean $+1.47$\,pp). Seeds with identical SC baselines (55.88\%) diverge ($+3.43$ vs.\ $0.00$\,pp), suggesting seed-level constraint quality matters (Appendix~\ref{app:entropy}). Mistral-7B on LogiQA ($\Delta = +0.50$\,pp, $p = 1.0$) shows the same SC range is insufficient when the domain resists formalization (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}).
67	
68	\paragraph{Type A (SC $\geq$ 60\%): confirmation bias.} All nine Type~A conditions (SC $\geq 60\%$, combined $N > 2{,}800$) produce no significant positive effects. The largest positive trend is LLaMA-3.1-8B on StrategyQA ($\Delta = +2.00$\,pp, $p = 0.424$, n.s.), while Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter shows significant degradation ($\Delta = -2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$). Even in-context learning with gold FOL examples ($\Delta = +0.50$\,pp, n.s.) cannot break confirmation bias.
69	
70	\paragraph{Type C (SC $\leq$ 40\%): error amplification.} Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($n = 600$, SC $= 39.33\%$) shows $\Delta = -2.83$\,pp ($p = 0.014$): SICA \emph{significantly hurts} by formalizing incorrect reasoning.
71	
72	\paragraph{Degenerate cases.} When $C_0 < 5\%$, nearly all traces agree and SICA reduces to SC by construction.
73	
74	\paragraph{Equivalence testing.} TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\pm 2$\,pp establish equivalence for 4 of 15 non-degenerate conditions; 5 show genuine SICA--SC differences (4 negative, 1 positive n.s.); the remaining 6 are underpowered (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}).

        "content": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models on three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations across ${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances. Beyond accuracy comparisons, we conduct diagnostic analyses of symbolic confirmation bias and process-level trace independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). Z3 timeout: 10s. $\\alpha{=}0.5$. on FOLIO (SC $\\sim$50--55\\%) shows positive directional trends in 2 of 4 seeds ($\\Delta = +3.43$\\,pp, $p = 0.092$; $+2.94$\\,pp, $p = 0.070$); no seed reaches $p < 0.05$ (4-seed mean $+1.47$\\,pp). Seeds with identical SC baselines (55.88\\%) diverge ($+3.43$ vs.\\ $0.00$\\,pp), suggesting seed-level constraint quality matters (Appendix~\\ref{app:entropy}). Mistral-7B on LogiQA ($\\Delta = +0.50$\\,pp, $p = 1.0$) shows the same SC range is insufficient when the domain resists formalization (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}).\n\n\\paragraph{Type A (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%): confirmation bias.} All nine Type~A conditions (SC $\\geq 60\\%$, combined $N > 2{,}800$) produce no significant positive effects. The largest positive trend is LLaMA-3.1-8B on StrategyQA ($\\Delta = +2.00$\\,pp, $p = 0.424$, n.s.), while Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter shows significant degradation ($\\Delta = -2.50$\\,pp, $p = 0.011$). Even in-context learning with gold FOL examples ($\\Delta = +0.50$\\,pp, n.s.) cannot break confirmation bias.\n\n\\paragraph{Type C (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%): error amplification.} Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($n = 600$, SC $= 39.33\\%$) shows $\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp ($p = 0.014$): SICA \\emph{significantly hurts} by formalizing incorrect reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{Degenerate cases.} When $C_0 < 5\\%$, nearly all traces agree and SICA reduces to SC by construction.\n\n\\paragraph{Equivalence testing.} TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp establish equivalence for 4 of 15 non-degenerate conditions; 5 show genuine SICA--SC differences (4 negative, 1 positive n.s.); the remaining 6 are underpowered (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\nOracle variants replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations: FOL from FOLIO ($n = 204$) and DSL from ProofWriter ($n = 600$). Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter) of problems. The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted result ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling, with ${\\sim}$88\\% lost to extraction quality.\n\n\n",

        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors. Recent extensions---CLOVER \\citep{ryu2025clover}, DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, VERGE \\citep{singh2026verge}, LogicAgent \\citep{luo2025logicagent}---add compositional translation, solver selection, decomposition, trained verifiers, and formal refinement, each introducing capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue that logical soundness is not a reliable criterion for neurosymbolic verification with LLMs. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, paralleling our confirmation bias finding (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. We extend this by quantifying \\emph{why} self-correction fails---same-model traces are correlated voters ($K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapses)---and showing this persists even under formal verification (Z3-checked symbolic constraints) and at the process level (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).",
        "numLines": 14,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 14
      }
    },

{
  "parentUuid": "f80b8947-1249-42ee-978d-8bf66a0aff3c",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts symbolic constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract symbolic constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.\nWhile prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: gold logical annotations yield $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the same-model ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter, $p = 0.013$; confirmed across three NLI architectures) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
        "numLines": 39,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 39
      }
    },
